## 2010 National Germany Championship

![](https://i.ormos.uk/12/62/1262895e327e0f345f23a0d3b0944260e35310dbf06c6dc34d97e82e78b98d95.jpg)

On a sunny Sunday morning in Aschaffenburg, Dennis Johannsen's smile outshone the midday sun as he received the winner's trophy for the German Nationals 2010. Three years after a second place finish his wish had finally come true: Leading the German National team at the World Championships. It was also Johannsen's twelfth Nationals attendance, which made him the player with the most German Nationals ever under his belt.

Come Chiba, Johannsen will be joined by a strong team. Jörg Unfried, 2006 team member, is looking forward to his return to the Worlds stage. And finalist Tobias Gräfensteiner is ready to seize this opportunity for parity with his brother Daniel, who set the bar with his Top 8 appearance at Pro Tour: San Diego. The two brothers squared off in the quarterfinals, where Tobias defeated his older brother in five games.

Of course, being brothers, they were card-perfect on each other's decklist – small wonder, as they played the exact same 75 cards. The deck they played ascended through the standings until all five pilots finished inside the Top 32, including the 2009 National Champion Sebastian Thaler. It is a regular [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) deck, but includes a transformational sideboard that brings in [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) and [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn). The plan worked, as the two Gräfensteiner brothers could well attest to on Sunday morning.

German Nationals 2010 is in the books, Grand Prix: Göteborg and Pro Tour: Amsterdam are next up on the schedule. Join us then and there here on dailymtg.com – and next year for German Nationals 2011!

*   [**Follow German-language coverage at planetmtg.de**](http://www.planetmtg.de/articles/artikel.html?id=5488)

## Quarterfinal: Florian Pils vs. Jörg Unfried

*by Hanno Terbuyken*

Florian Pils is already qualified for Worlds in Chiba, but that didn't stop him from aiming for the Nationals title. Jörg Unfried was also highly motivated to take a slot on the team for Japan, if only to remedy himself for Worlds 2006. Four years ago, Unfried was left hanging as the rest of the German National team got themselves disqualified.

### Game 1:

Unfried came blazing out of the gates on curve in the first turns: [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech), [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth) – Unfried had gas enough. Unfortunately for him, gas prices went through the roof as Pils had [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring), [Condemn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Condemn) and [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) in turn to stop Unfried in his tracks – despite taking a mulligan to six cards. [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) promised to rebuild Pils' ressources, but Jace met [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) after after brainstorming once, and the battlefield was barren again.

Unfried saw an opening and presented [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth), which stuck, gaining four life. While Pils was drawing cards with [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity), Unfried was putting the pressure on. Attacking with Lavaklauen-Gebiet and the Baloth, Pils used his [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) to remove the land, but went to 15 from the Baloth's attack.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ec/35/ec3569779001f1341cde07dad320292dbd704b56bf723d6aa084cec67f6a171d.jpg)*Florian Pils vs. Jörg Unfried*

Unfried knew that victory for him lay on the battlefield, not in drawing cards. [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) joined his ranks, but had to expend its energy to kill Pils' second [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor). Unfried's next play was [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander). Apparently, Pils' U/W deck had severe hiccups, as it failed to cough up a way to deal with the Siege-Gang.

Florian Pils 0 – 1 Jörg Unfried

Pils sideboarded IN 2 [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge), 1 [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity) and OUT 2 [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren), 1 [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity).

Unfried sideboarded IN 3 [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade), 3 [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress), 1 [Slave of Bolas](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slave+of+Bolas), and OUT 3 [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), 2 [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth), 1 [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast), 1 [Terminate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminate).

### Game 2:

Unfried equalled out the mulligan count and proceeded sedately with [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) on turn three. Pils shrugged, let the critter resolve and dispatched it with [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring). Tapped out, Pils had no chance to stop Unfried's [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). Cascade revealed [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress), which saw [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment), [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive), [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity), [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island), [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains). Unfried decided to deny Pils potential options and to keep him from gaining more unknown information, so he took the card drawer. Pils went to 17 from the attack.

Unfried tried [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) to get [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) off the Thrinax. Pils countered that with [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive). Another [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) from Unfried revealed another [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse), and now the Thrinax did come back. Pils tried to block with Celestial Column, which fell to Doomblade. Pils fell to 6 life. He held [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) still in hand, but also [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), and had to decide which one to play. The white sorcery would kill both Bloodbraid Elves and the [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), but also leave three Saproling tokens in play that Pils still would have to deal with.

On the other hand, Pils also had [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) in play, which would be enough to keep him alive through a full-on attack, so Pils decided on [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel). Even after Unfried added [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) to his side, Pils would stay alive. But Unfried had [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning), and Pils discarded the two lands he held together with [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment). He had staved off that attack and turned his [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) sideways to gain some breathing room. He could afford to leave himself open that way because his next play was – [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel)! Even through the floor, the crowd in the video room could be heard.

"Sorry," Unfried said, and the crowd went ballistic as he pointed his own topdeck, [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse), at the [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel)s.

Florian Pils 0 – 2 Jörg Unfried

### Game 3:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cf/62/cf629adbcc194dbbf4ae9798d8d5eb5591deb31b4a956970dbffc9dbc01db5aa.jpg)*Jörg Unfried*

Jörg Unfried expressed severe discontent at his opening hand and shuffled it back into his library. With six cards on the draw, Unfried opened with [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress). Pils' decision to play first paid back, as he had the mana to [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak)[Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress). [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) was next from Pils and the game finally seemed to go his way.

Catching a glimpse of Pils' hand, he was well set up with [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment), [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) and even [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge) to remove [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech). Unfried tried [Master of the Wild Hunt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+the+Wild+Hunt). Pils picked the card up and read it thorougly, then pointed [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) at it.

Turn five [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) from Pils looked solid, but Unfried's [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) took away [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment), a land and three life and Doomblade slayed the bane of [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel). Both players were now playing from the top.

[Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) and [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) from Unfried marched to the red zone, blocked by [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) and [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens). Pils waited until Unfried had pumped the Leech and played [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) to save his Wall.

Strong on removal, Pils used [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) to kill the Ravine a turn later, and the [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) successfully prevented Unfried's new hasty [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) from attacking, though Cascade gave him [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning). Pils fell to 15. Another turn later, a second [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) revealed [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), which Pils had a freshly drawn [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) for.

Still, the two Bloodbraid Elves were one too many for [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens), and they pecked away at Pils' life. But [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) let Pils check the top card of his library and build up loyalty counters, essentially creating a virtual blocker. Unfried wanted to kill that Jace, so both Elves attacked Jace. [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) blocked one, though [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) finished the Planeswalker.

15, 12, 9 – Florian Pils' life totals were dominated by the three-power Bloodbraid Elves. Like any good control deck, though, Pils had card drawing. Jaces's Ingenuity gave him a second [Wall of Omens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Omens) and protection from [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), as well as [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) and [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) off the Wall.

Now all Pils had to do was win somehow. [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) took a chunk of four out of Unfried's total, down to 11, then to 7. Unfried had [Terminate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminate) against the Colonnade, which Pils let resolve, because [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan) came his way and returned the land! Unfried's removal was not exhausted yet, though, with Doomblade killing the Titan. With two cards left in his hand, Unfried fell to 3 from the Colonnade before used Doomblade on it. That one also went through, and Pils simply reloaded with [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), saving his [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak).

But he didn't need it: Unfried had nothing and died.

Florian Pils 1 – 2 Jörg Unfried

### Game 4:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/24/29/2429f50a157253b4ee5a3ca7cbbe8f0ca54b8cd0af04ea3da0f1df9a5762e9f2.jpg)*Florian Pils*

This time, Pils went to Paris – twice, because in his six cards, he found three [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel)s and decided that was far too clunky. Unfried's start was [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) turn two. Pils had [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) for it, with the rest of his hand being [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity), [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan), [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) and [Glacial Fortress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glacial+Fortress). A tough situation when Unfried player [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning). Pils discarded [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) and [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), dropping to 17 life.

Unfried continued the slaying with [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) into [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress), revealing that he had drawn another [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile). Even though Pils had just three land in play, Unfried decided to get rid of the five mana card drawer, and the remaining [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) took out [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf).

Unfried had enough mana to smash in with [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine), drew [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) of the top and took the life totals to 16-13 in his favor. [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) joined the battlefield, while Pils was unable to draw a fourth land for four consecutive turns. 14-6 stood the life totals in Unfried's favor before Pils had [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) for [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax). Still, [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) took him to 2.

After missing six land drops in a row, Florian Pils had no chance to survive the onslaught that was the unsatisfying game four, and Jörg Unfried advanced to the semifinals!

Florian Pils 1 – 3 Jörg Unfried

  

## Quarterfinal – Sebastian Potyka vs. Dennis Johannsen

*by Tobias Henke*

Potyka won the die-roll, but "lost" one of his opening seven. He didn't seem too excited about his six-card hand either, but kept anyway. Meanwhile, Johannsen thought long and hard and sighed and finally kept a very land-heavy hand.

Potyka mutterred something about his "slow draw", laying four lands in a row before casting his first spell. However, the first spell was in fact two spells: [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) plus [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning). Johannsen went down to 14 and proceeded to cast just another land search spell.

A second [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) missed ([Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse)), but connected on the attack to bring Johannsen to 8. Johannsen summoned [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) and got a pair of [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle) onto the battlefield. Too late! Potyka smoothly cast a third [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) got in for 6 damage and clinched the first game with [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt).

Sebastian Potyka 1 – 0 Dennis Johannsen

Johannsen sideboarded [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth) and, unsurprisingly, Potyka took out his [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning)s.

And again, Potyka had to mulligan. This time, both players had a quick draw, Potyka with [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech)es on turn two and three, Johannsen with [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth) and [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate). With six mana available on turn four, he was certainly hoping for [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan). All he got, though, was [Avenger of Zendikar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avenger+of+Zendikar), and that had to wait in his hand for one more turn. The [Leeches](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Leeches) smashed in again and, with a little help from [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), brought Johannsen down to 5. The Avenger came down and planted eight Plant tokens on the battlefield.

[Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) got rid of extraneous vegetation and one Leech came through for another 4. Johannsen had no play and just passed the turn right back. [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) was already enough to finish him off at that point, but to add insult to injury its cascade ability also revealed [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) to kill the Avenger.

Sebastian Potyka 2 – 0 Dennis Johannsen

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2e/6c/2e6c3ed012406e1d1cb3da1ac9367742e3bb0427d8ed8ceaeef4e878fd4137dd.jpg)*Sebastian Potyka*

A good omen for Potyka: for the third time in a row he started the game with six cards. Johannsen began with [Terramorphic Expanse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terramorphic+Expanse) and [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle), having no two mana available on turn two. Potyka's second-turn [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress), however, revealed two [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan)s and two [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition)s in Johannsen's hand, along with one [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate), which went to the grave.

While Potyka summoned [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech), Johannsen's green spells worked their ***Magic***, and they would have gotten him to six mana in record time, if it wasn't for Potyka's kicked [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster). All Johannsen could do was to deploy a second and third [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition) (he had already used up the first one) and hope for the best.

Potyka's attack only got him down to 2. Johannsen summoned [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) and cracked his [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition)s. Two Expeditions equal four [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s, two Valakuts equal 24 damage.

Sebastian Potyka 2 – 1 Dennis Johannsen

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7c/c2/7cc282ee6634187e0f98e16013a22b01daad2c05e738e26ad8345d558a93afc7.jpg)*Dennis Johannsen*

The fourth game saw Johannsen mulligan extensively for a change. "First no land, and then no green source," Johannsen complained.

"You're playing 28 of them. Maybe you should have just kept," joked Potyka.

But all mulligans aside, Johannsen's draw was quite good, especially when compared to Potyka's, who had no plays for the first four turns and then an underwhelming [Sarkhan the Mad](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sarkhan+the+Mad). On the other side of the table, Johannsen made [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition) on turn two, [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate) on turn four, [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) on turn five, and killed his opponent on turn six.

Sebastian Potyka 2 – 2 Dennis Johannsen

Before the final game, there was some additional sideboarding: Potyka brought back [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning).

The fifth and final game saw both decks back in full swing. Potyka had [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) and [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress), Johannsen [Explore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explore) and [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow), revealing a hand of more [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow), [Rampaging Baloths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampaging+Baloths) and two [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan)s. [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) came down on turn four, fetched two Valakuts and died to [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade). End of turn, Potyka cast two [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt)s to put Johannsen at 6. At this point, one more Bolt, [Goblin Ruinblaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Ruinblaster), [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning), or [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) would spell doom for Johannsen. Potyka slowly looked at his topdeck and visibly sagged. Johannsen went down to 2 and summoned his second [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) to take the game and match.

Sebastian Potyka 2 – 3 Dennis Johannsen

  

## Semifinal – Christian Hauck vs. Tobias Gräfensteiner

*by Hanno Terbuyken*

The home stretch! Christian Hauck had started to play tournament ***Magic*** just a year ago, and already, he had a crack at winning Germany's most prestigious title with his Naya deck centered on [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman). Tobias Gräfensteiner, on the other hand, looks to equalize with his brother Daniel, whom he beat in the mirror match in the quarterfinal. The Ascension deck that both brothers ran featured a transformational sideboard into [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph), and the expectation was that Gräfensteiner would beat Hauck.

### Game 1:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7d/17/7d179111f592a942afa5e3a08132af43bb2145ed2e550639a8c317bdeec2da90.jpg)*Tobias Gräfensteiner*

Tobias Gräfensteiner won the die roll, and before Hauck had anything but land in play, Gräfensteiner already had [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) in play and played [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond), followed by [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain), putting all the cards he escried on the bottom of his library. Hauck's first action of the match was [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) into [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), to little effect as Gräfensteiner killed the elf with [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt).

Gräfensteiner spent two [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) to prevent [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), and a third [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) gunned for Hauck's second Knight the next turn. Hauck paid the three extra mana, tapping out, and was rewarded with the resolution of his 4/4 Knight.

Pressure was Hauck's game as he added [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) to the board, cascading into [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar). [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) joined the fray to remove one of Gräfensteiner's two Plant tokens (courtesy of [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden)s). Hauck attacked, with 6/6 Knight and [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). Calmly, Gräfensteiner dispatched the Knight with double-kicked [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) and threw his last Plant in [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf)'s way.

Still, Hauck was on the forward move. Attacking with [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) and [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) took Gräfensteiner to new lows, and [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) came to Hauck's side to finish Gräfensteiner off.

Christian Hauck 1 – 0 Tobias Gräfensteiner

Gräfensteiner sideboarded IN 1 [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn), 2 [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze), 4 [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph), 4 [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath), and sideboarded OUT 4 [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension), 3 [Foresee](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Foresee), 3 [Time Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Warp), 1 [Call to Mind](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+to+Mind).

Hauck sideboarded IN 1 [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage), 1 Quasali Pridemage, 1 [Realm Razer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Realm+Razer), and OUT 1 [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), 1 [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth), 1 [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe).

### Game 2:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/96/87/9687a7f3217de641ef0b9d9fe24cb4dae85cb86fae5ab190601fe2380ca946ed.jpg)

Gräfensteiner chose to play first, Hauck chose to mulligan. [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath), [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) and [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) for Gräfensteiner set the stage for turn four [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph), revealing Emrakul, and that was the second game gone in seconds, as Hauck was done for.

Christian Hauck 1 – 1 Tobias Gräfensteiner

Both players shuffled their entire sideboards into their decks, then took 15 cards out again, masking any eventual changes. In actuality, none of the players changed their decks at all from what they had sideboarded after game one.

### Game 3:

Again, Hauck mulliganed, then kicked off with [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) and [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman). Gräfensteiner filtered with [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) and [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder), then dismissed Hauck's [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) with [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning). Hauck hit [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) with an unlucky [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), played Quasali Pridemage and watched one of his Hierarchs die to [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath). Gräfensteiner tapped four mana and exchanged his plant for Emrakul once more with [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph).

Hauck untapped. What could he do? Not much but pass the turn. Annihilator 6 got rid of all his land and Quasali Pridemage, and Emrakul took him to 4. What scarce hope Hauck had passed quickly, and Gräfensteiner took game three.

Christian Hauck 1 – 2 Tobias Gräfensteiner

Hauck changed nothing in his deck, Gräfensteiner however took out the second [Call to Mind](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+to+Mind) and put a [Foresee](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Foresee) back into his deck.

### Game 4:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/8c/70/8c70658ef1685a91837e96f396a54cfc760374a59f7c84dbd080bbcb48264c55.jpg)*Christian Hauck*

Hauck hoped for his first play on the play, [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) to stick, but no: [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) took care of that. [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) from Gräfensteiner countered [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), gaining Gräfensteiner a little bit of tempo, just as [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath) on Hauck's new [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) did. [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) countered [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage). Gräfensteiner was trying to protect his Eldrazi Spawn token while trying to find [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) with [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) and [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond).

Hauck was short on mana, as Gräfensteiner's tactic of dispatching all the acceleration worked out well. Hauck tried [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) twice in a row, but with Into The Roil and [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning), Gräfensteiner kept the Shaman away.

Hauck hadn't drawn a land in four turns straight, and when he finally found a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) on top of his library, he was quick to play it and tap four lands for [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). Cascade... and the first card was another [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman)!

Gräfensteiner stayed his course. [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) countered [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), Into The Roil with Kicker took temporary care of the [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) before [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) sent it off the field. [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) for [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder), Gräfensteiner dug deeper into his library, looking for [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph). Hauck saw his out in getting damage on the table, and hard-cast [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) to take Gräfensteiner to 15, followed by a second [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) that Gräfensteiner had to block with his Plant token. At 11 life, Gräfensteiner had to deal with double [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine).

The way to do that is to play [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) before your last token dies as a chump-blocker, and that's exactly what Gräfensteiner did. [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) thundered to the battlefield. Hauck attacked into it with his two [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s on some forlorn hope that Gräfensteiner wouldn't block. Gräfensteiner went to 7, then Emrakul attacked and cleared Hauck's board save for a lonely forest, and that was it! Tobias Gräfensteiner was the first finalist of German Nationals 2010!

Christian Hauck 1 – 3 Tobias Gräfensteiner

  

## Semifinal – Dennis Johannsen vs. Jörg Unfried

*by Tobias Henke*

One more (and last) time the match-up is Valakut Ramp versus Jund. Dennis Johannsen had just dispatched the other Jund deck in his quarterfinal and was rather optimistic about his chances here. But then again, he was optimistic anyway: "I am still undefeated with this deck. 7-0-2 now."

The first game went extremely unfortunate for Unfried, with a mulligan and subsequent mana screw. Even stuck on one [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) and one [Swamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swamp), the amiable Unfried was good-humored about it, whistling some unidentifiable tune while Johannsen went through the motions and finished him in short order.

Dennis Johannsen 1 – 0 Jörg Unfried

This time around, Unfried had all three colors right from the start, with [Savage Lands](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Savage+Lands) and [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine). Johannsen, meanwhile, made [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle), [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest), and [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth) for his first of what should better be a lot of [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s.

Unfried summoned [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), Johannsen [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth). Unfried had a Baloth for himself, then [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) to kill Johannsen's, and attacked for 7 damage. Johannsen fell from 24 to 17, and to 16 when he used [Verdant Catacombs](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Verdant+Catacombs).

Next up, Johannsen went one size larger with [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan). He already had two Valakuts on the battlefield before but, even with some titanic help, only managed to get to three [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s. Things got even worse for him, when Unfried gleefully cast [Slave of Bolas](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slave+of+Bolas). He took the Titan and attacked for 13 damage.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d3/86/d38644f3f4438afd237ef91613ab27dd25ce1282955228d0536ee58c5748d664.jpg)*Dennis Johannsen*

Johannsen had [Avenger of Zendikar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avenger+of+Zendikar) and passed the turn. "Do you have anything?" he asked.

"Not yet," Unfried answered truthfully, "but there are sooo many cards in my deck now."

After drawing his card, he started counting his lands. Johannsen let out a sigh of relief: "So, that's at least no direct damage, then."

"Actually, it is," Unfried replied, revealing [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander), "I just needed to make sure I actually had the nine mana."

Dennis Johannsen 1 – 1 Jörg Unfried

The next game was one of these very short affairs the Valakut / Jund match-up seems to come up with from time to time. All of Unfried's lands entered the battlefeld tapped, so his first play was [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) on turn four. On the other side of the table, Johannsen had [Avenger of Zendikar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avenger+of+Zendikar), seven Plant tokens, with [Verdant Catacombs](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Verdant+Catacombs) and [Terramorphic Expanse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terramorphic+Expanse) still up and ready to boost the vegetation to enormous proportions. All by turn five. Unfried cast [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) looking for [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) and missed.

Dennis Johannsen 2 – 1 Jörg Unfried

A quick start for both: [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) and [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) for Unfried, [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition) and [Explore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explore) for Johannsen. [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) revealed the latter to be holding no noncreature spell, but also no more lands.

Without land, Johannsen could not cast the [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth) in his hand, instead he needed to spend his turn on the topdecked [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate).

Unfried's seven-power army went into the red zone again to put him at 2. If Johannsen drew an untapped land now, he would be able to summon two [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth)s and survive the onslaught. He drew [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle). One Baloth was not nearly good enough. Unfried had the removal and attacked for the win.

Dennis Johannsen 2 – 2 Jörg Unfried

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1b/29/1b29148b58680b6041c24550f825801c2229d810a2e03b6e0df186feea7709ff.jpg)*Jörg Unfried*

Unfried had the optimal start of turn two [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech), turn three [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax), but Johannsen's was even better. Much better, in fact, with [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition), [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow), and another [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition). By turn four, Johannsen controlled one [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle), two [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest)s and three [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s, as well as the remaining [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition) with two counters. He played another Valakut and summoned [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan). The Titan fetched a third Valakut and a fourth [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain).

"So, now you can shoot some of my creatures?" asked Unfried, innocently.

"Well, no. Now I just kill you."

[Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition) got another two [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s and each of these triggered all three Valakuts. (When the fifth and sixth [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) enter the battlefield simultaneously, they both "see" five other [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s.)

Two ([Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s) times three (damage) times three (from each Valakut) equals 18. And 18 life was exactly what his own [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) had left Unfried with. Not a perfect turn-four kill (not 20 damage), but a turn-four kill nevertheless!

Dennis Johannsen 3 – 2 Jörg Unfried

  

## Legacy Side Event – Top 8 Decklists

*by Tobias Henke*

Yesterday, 204 players joined the German Legacy Championships. Although the tournament was played with no Top 8 play-offs, the grueling nine rounds were finished well after the main event's last swiss round had already been concluded. That's why you had to wait till now to get these beautiful Top 8 decklists.
