## German Nationals 2010 Final: Dennis Johannsen vs. Tobias Gräfensteiner

*by Hanno Terbuyken*

Eight players gain Top 8 fame. Four players go to Worlds. Three players form the National Team. But only one player can be the 2010 National Champion. Dennis Johannsen or Tobias Gräfensteiner, [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) or [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn): Who would triumph in the end, and how? Read on to find out.

### Game 1:

Johannsen won the die roll, Gräfensteiner won a trip to Paris. Both players developed their game plan properly in the first couple of turns. Johannnsen with [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth) and [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow), Gräfensteiner with [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) and [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) on his second turn.

Johannsen had prepared the basic lands in his deck specially for the Top 8 matches. Little notes and shoutouts to the crowd that was watching the local broadcast in a nearby room were written on the lands, an extra source of entertainment in a match between two players very happy to fly to Worlds in Chiba this winter.

[Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition) helped Johannsen develop, as did [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) for Gräfensteiner, but Johannsen's quest progressed much faster than Gräfensteiner's. With the help of all that acceleration, Johannsen summoned [Rampaging Baloths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampaging+Baloths) to the battlefield.Gräfensteiner had no move but to point [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) at Johannsen's head. He was a little short on mana, and the 6/6 Baloth and friends took Gräfensteiner down in classic beatdown fashion.

Dennis Johannsen 1 – 0 Tobias Gräfensteiner

The players sideboarded. Dennis Johannsen brought IN 1 [Swamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swamp), 3 Thought Hemmorrhage and took OUT 1 [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), 1 [Rampaging Baloths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampaging+Baloths), 2 [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/66/94/6694280bc9e9af5b2bfdf1504e2685b45980a8e24a5c46993e6f4dc391558127.jpg)*Tobias Gräfensteiner*

Tobias Gräfensteiner brought IN 2 [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze), 3 [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath), 1 [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn), 4 [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) and took OUT 2 [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil), 4 [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning), 3 [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) and 1 [Time Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Warp).

### Game 2:

Both players kept their initial seven. Given that Gräfensteiner had announced "this wouldn't take long" before the finals, the Ascension player seemed to be not amused that he had lost the first game so easily. In game two, he drew first blood with [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath) on Johannsen, who had [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle), and [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition), all according to his gameplan.

As always after sideboarding, the question was: Where is [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph)? Looking at Gräfensteiner's hand, the answer was obvious: Right there, only the fourth land to play it was missing. [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) helped out, and [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) on a second [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition) was merely academical when Gräfensteiner played a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph), and [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) followed suit.

Johannsen sacrificed his loaded [Khalni Heart Expedition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Heart+Expedition), but with exactly six lands in play and nothing else, on 19 life, Emrakul was nigh unstoppable, as it were. Johannsen played [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate) and double [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth), trying to figure out if he could get some measure of relevant damage from [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle), but all in vain.

Dennis Johannsen 1 – 1 Tobias Gräfensteiner

Gräfensteiner went through the process of sideboarding once more, taking 2 [Foresee](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Foresee) out of his deck and replacing them with 1 [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath) and 1 [Time Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Warp). Johannsen also adjusted his deck, bringing the two [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow) back into the deck and taking out two [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate).

### Game 3:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cb/30/cb30639f1e8c1e755db93991ddf92056a8543ab7607ea0f7b3ff1d455c29aa30.jpg)*Dennis Johannsen*

Johannsen on the play began with a second-turn [Explore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explore) for an additional land drop, whereas Gräfensteiner had [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder), [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain), [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) as his first three plays in turns one and two. Surely, that had found him the combo pieces that he needed? Another [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) showed him [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) and land on top of his library, but he chose to shuffle them back – both [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) and [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath) were already in his hand. Sculpting the perfect hand was easy for Gräfensteiner with his cheap draw spells!

End of turn, Johannsen played [Summoning Trap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Summoning+Trap) for all the mana that he had, getting [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) for two [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle). Seven mana gave him [Avenger of Zendikar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avenger+of+Zendikar) and eight Plants – a good countermeasure for Emrakul's Annihilator 6 abilty. [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) attacked, found more [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s and those dealt 12 damage to Gräfensteiner. Johannsen felt confident to ward off Emrakul and passed the turn. But Gräfensteiner attacked and showed Johannsen the [Time Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Warp) he was holding. Staving off Emrakul twice was too much for Johannsen, and Gräfensteiner took game 3.

Dennis Johannsen 1 – 2 Tobias Gräfensteiner

### Game 4:

Gräfensteiner had to mulligan twice. The final five he kept: [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph), [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain), [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden), [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) and [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain). Quite a solid five. Gräfensteiner found [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), too, and countered Johannsen's [Explore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explore). [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) gave Gräfensteiner access to even more cards, but he failed to find the fourth land – yet. Ideally, he'd find land on top.

Johannsen, meanwhile, took his turn to ramp up with double [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth). Gräfensteiner drew... [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph)! [Call to Mind](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+to+Mind) returned his [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain), as Gräfensteiner desperately needed that fourth land. Every turn Johannsen got to develop his mana base even more was bad for Gräfensteiner. He passed the turn.

Johannsen looked at his board, counted the lands, and figured he had a comfortable amount. Six of those he used to pay for [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan), and Tobias Gräfensteiner instantly admitted defeat after almost pulling out of a mulligan to five.

Dennis Johannsen 2 – 2 Tobias Gräfensteiner

### Game 5:

So it came down to this! The players shook hands before the final game that stood between one of them and the winner's trophy.

This time, it was Johannsen's turn to mulligan. Johannsen set his hopes high: "Six cards, turn four kill!" But with Gräfensteiner on the play, he first had to watch as his opponent assembled part #1 of his combo: [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden) and the accompanying Plant.

Gräfensteiner had enough lands in hand, and a [Spawning Breath](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spawning+Breath), too, but he was still missing [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph). [Foresee](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Foresee) showed him [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain), [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond), Flasfreeze and [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) – not exactly the choice of cards he wanted as his next four. He would have to use the counters to stay alive long enough to see what lay beyond those four, and then there would have to be [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) within reach – not an easy task, as Johannsen kept on [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow)ing and Exploring.

The [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) Gräfensteiner used next turn, though, was a lucky one. Three cards down, Gräfensteiner found the [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) he needed! Now what was Johannsen's game with six lands available? And would Gräfensteiner's [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) help?

It wasn't needed – yet. All Johannsen did was lay [Evolving Wilds](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Evolving+Wilds). [Summoning Trap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Summoning+Trap) loomed, as Gräfensteiner couldn't play both [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) and [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak). He decided on [Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph), and Johannsen had the [Summoning Trap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Summoning+Trap) in response, finding [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan). Life totals were 19–19 as Johannsen searched for two [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle), from his library.

Johannsen: "Why can't you be on 18 life?"

![](https://i.ormos.uk/a3/6e/a36edb48687d192a6ac72e24078d5e5403749c83de77a33b82febdae5d805946.jpg)

[Polymorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Polymorph) resolved and brought [Emrakul, the Aeons Torn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emrakul%2C+the+Aeons+Torn) into play. Johannsen: "Come on, be the last card, so I know what's in your hand!" He carefully looked through the revealed cards. "If you don't have [Dispel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dispel), you are dead," Johannsen pondered.

He went to his turn, drew his card. "This is the ultra-win!" Johannsen had found a third [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle). And when he played [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow), Tobias Gräfensteiner extended the hand to congratulate his opponent, Dennis Johannsen, the German National Champion 2010!

"He never showed [Dispel](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dispel), that's why I boarded the [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow)s back in," explained a very happy Dennis Johannsen after the match. With two [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s from [Harrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harrow) and two more from an attack with [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan), Johannsen had been able to deal 36 damage on that final turn, and that was more than enough to catapult him over Emrakul's head to become this year's champion.

Dennis Johannsen 3 – 2 Tobias Gräfensteiner

  

## Decklists With Winning Records

*by Tobias Henke*

These are all Standard decks with a record of 6-2 (or better) from the first four rounds of day one and the last four rounds of day two. Two lists are particularly noteworthy. Remember the deck we mentioned early in the day one coverage? The one that won two [Last Chance](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Last+Chance) Qualifiers on Thursday? Apparently, some players managed to scrounge this list from none other than Pro Tour Hall of Famer Zvi Mowshovitz. One lucky man who ended up playing the deck was Michael Filler and, of course, he went 7-1 in the Standard portion.

The other list didn't perform quite as well, but was the talk of the tournament for some time over the weekend. The so-called "Neo-[Dredge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dredge)" or "[Dredge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dredge)-Vine". Just look for Lukas Diekjobst among the 6-2s.

#### Sebastian Potyka, 8-0

Exile";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

## Third Place Play-Off – Jörg Unfried vs. Christian Hauck

*by Florian Koch*

In the match for third place Jörg Unfried, playing Jund, met Christian Hauck, playing Naya. Unfried is well known in the German **Magic** community, a player who grinded himself into Pro Tours and Nationals before and even made the national team once. His opponent Christian Hauck only started playing competitive **Magic** a year ago.

Hauck was on the play and opened with [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) which immediately went down to [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt). The following [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) was a bit beafier, entering the battlefield as a 5/5. A [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) on the other side managed to stare down the Knight, though. But Hauck added [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) to his board, thus denying Unfried his fourth mana. Unfried answered with [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax). Then Hauck went deep into the tank before deciding to keep Unfried's land tapped and to add [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) to his board.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1d/74/1d745ee7684bb6279558c8d5b6e79cd7a186ce6bbc2e784905c6243b1ac15031.jpg)

Without a fifth land, Unfried chose to go for Ajani's throat despite the daunting Knight. His [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) got killed in the effort but Ajani went down to two counters. When Hauck played [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) to search for [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar), things started to look bad for Unfried. The Sparkmage / Collar combo assembled, he passed the turn. Unfried decided to ignore the combo for the time being, killing Ajana with [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) instead. The obligatory end-of-turn ping didn't go to his creatures anyway as Thrinaxes don't make for great targets. Ajani dead, Unfried could finally play [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and even cascaded into [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse). He destroyed the [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) and, this time, Hauck eliminated one of the Thrinaxes in response.

As it turned out, Hauck's Naya deck was not so straight Naya, splashing for the infamous [Sovereigns of Lost Alara](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sovereigns+of+Lost+Alara) one-card combo. A [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) thus crashed into Unfried for 15 points of damage. The Jund player had the [Terminate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminate) for the Eldrazi-Vine and succeeded in eliminating the threat as [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) was busy digging for blue mana. But as the players knew their opponent's decklists Unfried too knew that Hauck had another [Eldrazi Conscription](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Conscription) in his deck. His only hope was that Hauck had already drawn the other Conscription, but unfortunately for Unfried that was not the case.

Unfried 0 – 1 Hauck

Unfried sideboarded:

-4 [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning)

-4 [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech)

-2 [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth)

+3 [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar)

+4 [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage)

+3 [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade)

This time, Unfried was on the play, but Hauck had the first play, a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) which was taken down by Lightning. Unfried then played [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage), but that got [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge)d. Short on mana, Unfried had to say "go" once again. Hauck then put [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) and [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) onto the battlefield. While the Knight vanished in the Maelstrom, a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) joined the Cobra to attack Unfried. Finally finding his fourth mana, Unfried played [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and cascaded into [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade). The Cobra was sent packing and a turn later a newly played [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) met the same fate.

Hauck tried to put pressure on Unfried with [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine), but a lightning struck the land and the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) died to the blocking [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). A [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) alone was not enough to bring the Elemental back and now it was Unfried that put pressure on his opponent and even added a [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander) to the mix. However, a turn later, Hauck found yet another [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), cascaded into [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), and brought back [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine).

Unfried was a bit more fortunate with his cascade as he [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast)ed into [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), killing all of Hauck's capable blockers. Now on 4 life, Hauck folded to the invading Siege-Gang.

Unfried 1 – 1 Hauck

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e5/d5/e5d58f3a4c0edb618498a742d4f29d354c6be630d8f4c9e974de2be452b8326b.jpg)*Christian Hauck*

One more game Hauck began with [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch). [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) and [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) showed up as well, and one turn later [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). A swift start that Unfried could slow only by shooting down the Shaman. Before any real damage was done, Unfried send a [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax) on guard duty. His opponent didn't have [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge) this time, but played [Dauntless Escort](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dauntless+Escort). The Thrinax died anyway, but by blocking an attacking [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine). Unfried then played [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) and equipped a token. When Hauck managed to hard-cast an [Eldrazi Conscription](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Conscription), Unfried's seemed doomed anyway. Adding insult to injury, his opponent played Elspeth to really seal the deal.

Unfried 1 – 2 Hauck

And again it was [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman)'s turn to arrive first onto the battlefield. Termination was his fate and Unfried put a Thrinax onto the battlefield. Hauck had [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) which was a only 3/3, but Unfried had no solution for that one anyway. Instead he played [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage), while missing his fourth land drop. His opponent went for [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) again, but Unfried had his fourth land and could play [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and shoot the Shaman. [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) dug up a [Sejiri Steppe](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sejiri+Steppe) to save it. Apparently grateful the Shaman found a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and that one in turn cascaded into another [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). Full circle. Elves traded and suddenly Hauck had very respectable board to Unfried's lone [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage).

Unfried summoned a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) himself and cascaded into [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage). The duo took care of the [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), but the opposing pair of 7/7 Knights looked as if they would cause trouble soon. In turn Hauck created another [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge)d his opponent's [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) via cascade. Unfried played [Master of the Wild Hunt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+the+Wild+Hunt) to help his defense, but Hauck had even more [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). Stoically Unfried tried to hold up his defense as best as possible. Hauck managed to rip even his fourth Elf, but between chumpblocking and shooting Bloodbraid Elves with his Sparkmages, Unfried somehow still managed to squeeze in some damage with his [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine).

The next combat saw a Knight going down to [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade), while the other one was blocked by [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage). This time Unfried ripped [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) to take down the remaining Knight. And suddenly things looked bad for Hauck.

I honestly had punched in "that seals the deal" three times, but now Unfried attacked with [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) and Hauck had to decide whether to chumpblock with his newly summoned [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) or go to 3. He decided to take the hit and Unfried announced "Tot!", the German word for "dead". [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) took an epic game for Unfried that seemed to be over for turns running.

Unfried 2 – 2 Hauck

![](https://i.ormos.uk/bc/f3/bcf33ccebeb86199a19ad0fb3f839499d3590d256fff79b2b3a4abb221d2ce3c.jpg)*Jörg Unfried*

The final game began with no plays from Hauck for the first three turns. Unfried had [Basilisk Collar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Basilisk+Collar) and [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) assembled by that time. Eventually Hauck put a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) onto the battlefield, attacking. Unfried only equipped the mage and gave the turn back. What happened next seemed... strange. Hauck played [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), trying to go through the killing machine despite having [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) in his hand since the beginning of the game. He found [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) that way, but the Knight died to [Terminate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terminate). The next [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), having found only a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), was eliminated by the Sparkmage assassin as was his twin before. A turn later the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) went down, too. Now Hauck played Linvala and [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), but Unfried had [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) for the Angel and [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) for the Knight and quickly the board was wiped clean again.

Unfried finally played another creature, a [Sprouting Thrinax](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sprouting+Thrinax). Hauck found one more [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and both Elementals fell in combat, one blocked, one sniped. An [Obstinate Baloth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obstinate+Baloth) was able to recover them both, though. The next turn saw Baloth, two [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s, and [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) attacking, but the land was struck by lightning, the Baloth assassinated, and the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s ran into Saproling tokens. Meanwhile, a second [Cunning Sparkmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cunning+Sparkmage) took down the mana critters. Even as Hauck [Celestial Purge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Purge)d one of the Sparkmages, a third one entered the battlefield. Then both players took a moment to review Hauck's graveyard. It was a massacre. Eventually, Unfried began the counterattack. And when he played [Siege-Gang Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20170721050310/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Siege-Gang+Commander), the game soon was over.

Unfried 3 – 2 Hauck
